Course structure AC dated 28.03.2025 - additional agenda BSH dept pg 13
# Syllabus common for B Tech (all program) can use common question paper.
Engineering Ethics - This course focuses on instilling Engineering ethics, which are taught and assessed throughout the semester.
Engineering Workshop - The course is based on practical assignments only
‘Transforming Ideas to Innovation - This course introduces to engineering through multidisciplinary, real-world contexts while building skills in design, teamwork, problem-solving, and modem engineering tools.
Indian Knowledge System - This course enables students to integrate traditional indian wisdom with modern concepts.
Environmental Science - The course is for the general awareness about the environment. .

Computer Applications in Civil Engineering - I - Course is for learning the softwares in Civil Engineering from employability point of view. It is project based course not exam based. .

Interpersonal Skills - This 1 credit practical course requires rigorous formative and summative Internal assessment continuously, to ensure the students have acquired specific interpersonal skills, thereby eliminating the need for term end assessment.

Objective:

Prerequisite :

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

1. | Sources of Water : 4

Long term needs for towns/cities, surface and ground water sources,
domestic storage of rain waters in drought prone areas.

2, Quality of water and waste water : 10
Physical, Chemical, microbiological characteristic, IS:10500 limits,
laboratory analysis, significance of results, waterborne diseases; Quality
parameters of sewage — DO, BOD, COD; stream pollution, Streeter-
Phelp’s equation, examples.

3: Quantities of water and waste water : 4
Average per capita need of water with respect to various uses, factors
affecting quantity required and variation in demand, fire demand,
quantities of sewage generated.

4, Conveyance of water and wastewater : 6
Estimation of fluid flows, engineering requirements of conduits with
respect to their performance, various pipe materials, appurtenances,
associated problems.

5. Treatment processes for water and wastewater : 10
Particle sizes and suitability of treatment, primary, secondary and tertiary
treatments, options, mechanisms, parameters treated, treatment
efficiencies, design of various units, disposal.

7 } 6. Reuse, Recycle and Renovation : 6

Options and suitability, desalination, ground water recharge by rain water
and treated wastewater, limitations of conventional treatment, polishing
treatments.

Total 40

Various types of surveying — based on methods and _ instrument,

Linear measurements, approximate, direct, optical and electronic methods
Chain surveying, minor instruments for setting out right angle.

Bearings — different types, compass — prismatic, surveyor, whole circle and

Difficulties in leveling work, reduction of levels, correntions and
precautions in leveling work, problems.

Contour — definitions, contour interval, equivalent, uses and characteristics
of contour lines, direct and indirect methods of contouring
Running a level line, L section, cross section, methods of interpolation
\ Grade contour — definition, use setting out in field
Ldomsuttion of volume by trapezoidal and prismoidal formula, volume
from spot levels, volume from contour plan.

4.\ Areas : 03
Area of a irregular figure by Trapezoidal rule, average ordinate rule,

Various parts and axis of transit, technical terms, temporary and permanent
adjustments of a transit, horizontal and vertical angles, methods of

Sub : Seminar - I Code : S-I

Objective:
To develop ability to collect information and prepare a report for presentation on a
technical topic.

Prerequisite:
Basic Discipline, subjects upto Trimester IV.

Assignment of topic for the Seminar will be given to each of the students in the class by one of
the teachers teaching the Trimester V subjects. The students will prepare the detailed report on
the Seminar topic by literature survey / self study under the guidance of the concerned teacher

and submit the report for evaluation at the end of the Trimester V.

The report will cover the following :

1. Relevance and importance of the topic
2. Basic Principles / Concepts / Definitions

3. Details of development — Methods / Techniques / Data / Charts / Diagrams

4. Applications — Advantages and Limitations.
5. Discussion of related issues.

6. Future scope

Sub: STRENGTH OF MATERIALS Code:
Periods per week a 5 hrs.
Practical 2 Hrs.
Tutorial -
Hours Marks
Evaluation Scheme Theory Examination 3 100 (Scaled down to 50)
Test 1 20
Term work/Pr./Oral 30

Objective: The aim of the syllabus is to make the students conversant with various types of
forces/loading and the resultant stresses there on to which mechanical components and
structures are subjected to. All engineering structures do deform under the action of loads to
which they are subjected. These deformations are expected to be within acceptable limits. The
structural member is not expected to exceed its deformation limits or fail or fracture under
normal operating conditions. Thus the structural member is expected to be sufficiently strong,
stiff and stable while in operation. The study of these parameters viz. The strength, stiffness
and stability of the member under loading will be dealt in this syllabus of Mechanics of
Deformable bodies more commonly referred to as Strength of Materials.

Prerequisite | Course in Engineering Mechanics, Statistics

Detailed syllabus

Units Topics Duration (Hrs.)

Stress and Strain: Strain, modulus of elasticity, modulus of
rigidity, bulk modulus, yield stress, ultimate stress, factor of safety,
shear stress, Poisson’s ratio, analysis of members made of
composite materials.

1

Shear Force and Bending Moment: Axial force, shear force and
bending moment diagrams for statically determinate beams
(excluding beams with internal hinges) for different types of
loading.

; Simple Theory of Bending: Flexure formula for straight beams,
13 simple problems involving application of flexure formula, section 6
Hy) modulus, moment of resistance of a section.

La Shear Stress in Beams: Distribution of shear stress across plane
sections used commonly for structural purposes.

Simple Theory of Torsion: Torsion of circular shafts — solid and
5 hollow, stresses in power transmission shafts (excluding shafts in 3
series and parallel).

Bending Moment Combined with Axial Loads:
6 Application to members subjected to eccentric loads, core of a 3
section, problems on chimneys involving lateral loads.

2011-12
--- page 4 ---
SVKM’S NMIMS

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

Principal Stresses: General equations for transformation of stress,
stress on an oblique plane of a member subjected to General two
directional stress system. Principal planes and principal stresses,
maximum shear stress, Mohr’s circle.

Deflection of Beams: Deflection of cantilevers, simply supported
8 and overhanging beams using double integration and Macaulay’s 6
methods for different types of loading.

Struts: Struts subjected to axial loads, concept of buckling, Euler’s

9 . a a 7 4
and Rankine’s formula for struts with different support conditions.
Thin Shells: Thin cylindrical and spherical shells, volumetric
10 . «4s i ‘ A 4
strain, cylindrical shell with hemispherical ends.
Total 50

Sub: Principles and Practice of Management Code:
Periods per week Lecture | 3 hrs.
Term Work(Practical) | 9
Tutorial | -
Hours Marks
Evaluation Scheme Theory Examination 2 50
Term Work 50
Objectives:

To familiarize students on the following:

Management is the process of achieving organizational goals by engaging in the four major
functions of planning, organizing, leading, and controlling. Management entails reaching
important goals, and involves knowing how to perform the major functions of management. The
course aims at introducing the major functions of management and providing an overview on
activities within organizations. The course will provide an understanding of the concepts of
management in both profit & non-profit organizations

Prerequisite: Nil

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration (Hr)
1 Organizational culture, global perspectiv, social & responsibility 6
relating to the terrain of manager.
2 The Planning Process: Planning function of management and it’s 6

relationship to decision making, foundations of planning, strategic
management and various planning tools and techniques.

3 Organization: Understanding the organizing function of 6
management and it’s relationship to organizational structure,
communication, human resource management and managing

change

4 Managing People: Identify the leading function of management and 6
it’s relationship to organizational structure, communication, human
resource management and managing change. Identify the leading
interaction, employee motivation and the theories of leadership.

5 Control functions, operations and value chain management: 6
Foundations of the control function of management and the
concepts of operations and value chain management.

TOTAL 30

Prescribed text: Robbins, 9" edition, 2007, Prentice HALL 978-0-13-225773-2

References:

Bartol, K. and Martin, D. (1998): Management, Boston: Irwin McGraw-Hill.

Kotler, P. (2003): Marketing Management. Upper Saddle River, Prentice Hall.

The course is based on these two textbooks. Any additional reading material will be provided
by the lecturer.

Unit Topics

matrix structures; review of project life cycle, planning for achieving time, cost,

necessary documentation for major works like dam, multi-storied structures, ports,
tunnel, Qualities. Role, responsibility of projects Manager, Role of PMC (Project
Management Consultants) on major projects.

on Project and |

Duration

Managers.

expectations and performance models.

negotiation

arbitration lndian Arbitration Act, arbitration agreement, conduct of arbitration,
power and duties of arbitrator, rules of evidence/ preparation and publication of
awards, methods of enforcement, impeding and award. Limitations of arbitration in

advantages.

Unit Topics Duration
(Hrs)

10
iApproximate estimate: - importance, purposes, approximate methods oi building
estimating and various civil engineering works.

detailed estimate, factors to be consider during preparation of detailed estimate,
methods of taking out quantities, abstracting, units of measurements, building cost
index, prime cost, provisional sum, centage charges, work charged Establishment,
administrative approval, technical sanction

roof buildings.)

icolumn &lsolated column footing, staircase and preparation of bar bending schedule.

4 {Task work, factors affecting task work, schedule of rate, Task work of various items of 4
Construction, Analysis of rates, factors affecting cost of an item of work, material,
labour etc., Analysis of various items of construction.

5 Valuation, purposes, price cost and value, factors affecting value of a property, various 10
types of value like market value, sentimental value, mortgage, year’s purchase and
outgoings, legal aspects of valuation and easement act. Methods of valuation, land and
building method, rental method, belting method of valuation of land. Standard rent
and Standard rent fixation. Depreciation, various methods of depreciation, sinking
fund, book value, free hold and lease holds properties.

6 Genders & Contracts: Types, BOQ, Specification , submission of tenders, openings of 4

tenders, security and prequalification
